# Python and Jupyter Analyzer Specification

## 1. Identity, evidence boundary, and license

This specification covers the Python analyzer bundled with SonarQube Community Build `26.8.0.126808`. Its runtime coordinate is `org.sonarsource.python:sonar-python-plugin:5.27.0.35548`. The plugin manifest identifies plugin key `python`, display version `5.27 (build 35548)`, required languages `py,ipynb`, minimum Sonar API version `9.9`, minimum JRE version `11`, Java version `17`, and SonarLint support. The artifact was built on 2026-07-30. Evidence: `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`; `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/META-INF/maven/org.sonarsource.python/sonar-python-plugin/pom.xml`; [M1].

Authoritative immutable artifacts and SHA-256 values are:

- `org.sonarsource.python:sonar-python-plugin:5.27.0.35548` runtime JAR: `126234f10b1507e63055a028f8762fa80beaf078bbe07cbe4571a62cdecef952`.
- matching plugin source JAR: `fc0d3bca7053c873e7baf4150a491db1e0342b11f4685484781decc83926282d`.
- `org.sonarsource.python:python-frontend:5.27.0.35548` source JAR: `ef32d0e08a3945e8a37df6d4f19e5f728825a7f738bfedea656894d07d46068e`.
- `org.sonarsource.python:python-checks:5.27.0.35548` source JAR: `903d0721cedfbe580786e1c3657807f3f901cae7797fdab6a6fc4c1ab1bb42e1`.
- `org.sonarsource.python:python-commons:5.27.0.35548` source JAR: `2aef21dc226b14e53f6c66e9eb8a65fa0e29f7bdb7f5147cf5eb37c0c475b723`.

Evidence: [M1] through [M5].

Analyzer-owned source is licensed under Sonar Source-Available License v1.0, recorded here as `LicenseRef-SSAL-1.0`. Bundled notices also identify third-party LGPL, Apache, BSD, MIT, and related dependencies; those libraries are dependencies, not re-specified implementations. This is a provenance record, not legal advice. Evidence: `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/licenses/LICENSE.txt`; `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/licenses/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES/`; `manifest/license-map.md`.

### Provenance limitation

The published POM uses SCM tag `HEAD`; no matching public release tag cryptographically binds a Git tree to these artifacts. The runtime manifest records `Implementation-Build: 4a54c49ad283d374577989a2b280d03744008e12`, while candidate release-boundary commit `2c3590318c644163418f82b57341a21ac1b5cb99` is only corroborating evidence. This specification asserts behavior only when present in the exact immutable runtime or source artifacts listed above. Candidate-branch-only behavior is excluded. Evidence: `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`; `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/META-INF/maven/org.sonarsource.python/sonar-python-plugin/pom.xml`; `manifest/license-map.md`.

## 2. Purpose, scope, exclusions, and owned contracts

The analyzer statically recognizes Python files and Jupyter notebooks, builds syntax and semantic models, runs bundled or custom checks, imports supported reports, and emits scanner issues, analysis errors, measures, highlighting, symbols, duplication tokens, warnings, cache entries, and analyzer telemetry. It never needs to execute analyzed Python code. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/parser/PythonParser.java`.

This component owns language recognition, notebook extraction and source projection, Python parsing and semantic/type analysis, its rule repositories and default profiles, Python-specific metrics, CPD token production, source highlighting and symbol references, Python coverage and xUnit import, five external-linter import adapters, project package indexing, incremental analyzer cache behavior, and Python telemetry values.

It does not own scanner file indexing, generic plugin loading, active-rule and quality-profile persistence, issue lifecycle, scanner-report transport, metric persistence, compute-engine aggregation, quality gates, HTTP APIs, or telemetry upload policy. Those contracts belong respectively to `integrations/scanner-engine/`, `platform/plugin-extension-runtime/`, `platform/project-configuration/`, `platform/analysis-results/`, `platform/scanner-gateway/`, `platform/compute-engine/`, and `platform/telemetry-monitoring/`. Commercial `com.sonarsource.python:sonar-python-enterprise-plugin:5.27.0.35548` behavior is excluded even though the product build declares that separate artifact for other editions. Evidence: `manifest/dependency-map.md`; `manifest/component-inventory.md`; `manifest/release-baseline.md`.

Primary dependencies are the Sonar plugin API, SonarLint plugin API when available, analyzer-commons report/metadata facilities, SSLR lexer/parser support, safe XML parsing, JSON and TOML parsing, and the exact same-version `python-frontend`, `python-checks`, and `python-commons` modules. These dependencies provide platform or library mechanics; Python-specific behavior remains defined here. Evidence: `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/META-INF/maven/org.sonarsource.python/sonar-python-plugin/pom.xml`.

## 3. Language and file recognition

### 3.1 Python

Language key `py` is named “Python”. `sonar.python.file.suffixes` is a project-scoped multi-value setting with default `py`. Values are trimmed and blank values removed. If no nonblank configured suffix remains, the analyzer restores default `py`. File inclusion, source/test type, exclusions, encoding, and generated status continue to come from scanner file-system contracts. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/Python.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensionKeys.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`.

### 3.2 Jupyter/IPython notebooks

Language key `ipynb` is named “IPython Notebooks”. `sonar.ipynb.file.suffixes` is a project-scoped multi-value setting with default `ipynb`, with the same trim, blank filtering, and default-restoration behavior. The runtime repository key is `ipython`; it exposes the same Community rule classes under the notebook language. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynb.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbRuleRepository.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`.

A notebook is eligible when top-level `metadata.kernelspec.language` or `metadata.language_info.name` is exactly lowercase `python` or `ipython`. If either accepted value exists, it wins over an unsupported value found in the other field. Missing language metadata is treated as Python. Any other found language causes the notebook to be skipped without an analysis result. Cell-level language metadata is ignored. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IpynbNotebookParser.java`.

Only cells whose `cell_type` is `code` contribute source. Markdown, raw cells, outputs, execution counts, attachments, and other notebook fields do not. A code-cell `source` may be either a JSON string or an array of JSON strings; unexpected source token kinds invalidate that notebook. Empty notebooks and notebooks without a `cells` array yield an empty generated Python view. Evidence: same `IpynbNotebookParser.java`.

### 3.3 Test classification and precedence

Platform `InputFile.Type.TEST` always remains test input. A nonblank `sonar.tests` setting, `sonar.python.testFileHeuristic.disabled=true`, or the corresponding analyzer-commons heuristic disable switch bypasses Python’s fallback classifier. Otherwise a platform-main file is treated as test for rule applicability when its path contains a directory named `test` or `tests`, its filename starts with `test_` or ends with `_test.py`, it imports top-level `pytest` or `unittest`, or it contains a top-level function whose name starts with `test_` and whose body contains an assertion. This inferred type affects rule scopes only; metrics still use the platform file type. The analyzer emits at most one warning per analysis when this fallback changes a main file to test. The effective inferred type is cached for unchanged-file analysis. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/TestFileClassifier.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`.


## 4. Grammar and Python-version behavior

`sonar.python.version` is a project-scoped multi-value setting. Exact major/minor mappings are `3.0` through `3.14`; values `3.0` through `3.7` use the `3.8` type-stub model, while `3.8` through `3.14` retain their respective model. A lone `3` selects all supported versions. When a value has more than two dot-separated components, only major and minor are parsed; trailing components are ignored. Thus a patch version or even a malformed trailing component still selects the mapped major/minor model and emits the unsupported-version warning. Multiple values form a deduplicated set. Missing configuration selects all supported models, `3.8` through `3.14`, and emits a server-side analysis warning recommending an exact version; SonarLint does not emit that warning. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/api/PythonVersionUtils.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonSensor.java`.

For a numeric version with major 3 but minor below `3.8`, the model is clamped to `3.8`; a numeric version above `3.14` is clamped to `3.14`, with a warning. A malformed major or minor component, or a major version below 3, causes the entire property parse to fall back to all supported versions. A trailing malformed component does not. A failing member of a multi-value setting therefore discards earlier accumulated precision, while a member with valid major/minor and any suffix contributes its mapped model. The selected set filters versioned Typeshed symbols and is exposed to rules; compatibility rules may use it. Evidence: same `PythonVersionUtils.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/v2/typeshed/ProtoUtils.java`.

The lexer/parser does not switch grammar according to `sonar.python.version`. One static grammar accepts legacy constructs needed to diagnose older Python, together with current constructs including asynchronous syntax, formatted strings, template strings, assignment expressions, structural pattern matching, exception groups, type aliases, type parameters and defaults, and Python 3.14 multiple exception expressions. Parser acceptance therefore does not itself prove configured-version compatibility; version-sensitive rules and the semantic library model provide that distinction. The lexer emits indentation and dedentation, inserts final dedentations before end-of-file, and maintains separate notebook-cell handling. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/api/PythonGrammarBuilder.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/api/PythonGrammar.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/lexer/PythonLexer.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/parser/PythonParser.java`.

Notebook grammar adds code-cell delimiters, line magics, cell magics, shell-style lines, slash-style line magics, and dynamic object-information statements. Cell magic consumes content until the next notebook delimiter or end-of-file. These constructs are syntax accommodations; they are not executed. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/api/IPythonGrammarBuilder.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/api/IPythonGrammar.java`.

## 5. Notebook transformation and location mapping

For server analysis, notebook JSON is transformed into one in-memory Python stream. Code cells retain order and are separated by an analyzer-only delimiter. Source-array elements that omit a trailing newline continue the current generated line; embedded newlines inside one JSON string are split into generated lines. A final newline is added when required for parsing. No transformed file is written as a user-visible source file. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IpynbNotebookParser.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/GeneratedIPythonFile.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/NotebookParsingData.java`.

Each generated line records its original JSON line and column, whether the notebook is compressed onto one physical JSON line, and offsets caused by JSON escaping. Syntax nodes, issues, secondary locations, highlighting, symbols, measures, and analysis errors use the original notebook file. Precise ranges are projected through the mapping. For a line-level issue in a compressed notebook, the range extends from that generated line’s mapped point to the next mapped point; an absent next point is an internal consistency error. Noncompressed line-level locations select the original JSON line. Parser errors replace the generated line number in the message and pointer with the mapped notebook line. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/api/IssueLocation.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/IPythonLocation.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`.

Server-side notebook caching is disabled, notebook files do not enter the project-level global symbol table, and notebook CPD tokens are not emitted. Notebook executable-line data is empty, and per-line `ncloc_data` is not emitted, although file-level measures and source presentation still use mapped syntax. SonarLint may supply already-preprocessed notebook content and uses its available indexer instead of server JSON extraction. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PythonIndexer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/MeasuresRepository.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/FileLinesVisitor.java`.

## 6. Semantic and type model

After lexing and parsing, the analyzer constructs a typed syntax tree. Symbol construction follows Python name-binding scopes for modules, classes, functions, lambdas, comprehensions, parameters, imports, assignments, global and nonlocal declarations, patterns, exception targets, with targets, and type parameters. It runs multiple binding and resolution phases, records usages and binding kinds, attaches global/local/class/instance fields to trees, creates alternative symbols for ambiguous repeated class/function definitions, resolves inheritance where evidence exists, and then runs type inference. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/SymbolTableBuilder.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/Scope.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/SymbolImpl.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/AmbiguousSymbolImpl.java`.

The project-level model assigns fully qualified module names from resolved package roots and file paths, indexes public project descriptors and import relationships, and combines project symbols with bundled, Python-version-filtered Typeshed descriptors. Imports that cannot be resolved remain unknown rather than causing analysis failure. A project-level type table caches resolved types. Notebooks are intentionally excluded from this cross-file table. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PythonIndexer.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/ProjectLevelSymbolTable.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/v2/typeshed/TypeShedDescriptorsProvider.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/semantic/v2/typetable/ProjectLevelTypeTable.java`.

Type inference propagates assignment and annotation types, resolves member access against inferred receiver types, and uses control-flow-sensitive analysis for module and function bodies. The flow pass runs twice so member resolution from the first pass can refine the second. Because the control-flow model does not precisely model try/except, a body containing a try statement uses AST-based propagation instead. Unknown, ambiguous, union-like, unresolved-import, runtime-builtin, and declared types are representable outcomes. Rules must treat missing semantic evidence conservatively according to their cataloged exclusions. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/types/TypeInference.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/types/InferredTypes.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/cfg/ControlFlowGraphBuilder.java`.

The analyzer exposes a custom-rule extension point whose provider supplies a repository key and annotated check classes. Custom repositories share parsing, issue emission, scopes, end-of-analysis callbacks, and per-repository locking, but their rule catalog is not part of this bundled specification. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/api/PythonCustomRuleRepository.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonChecks.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`.


## 7. Project indexing and package resolution

Package-root resolution recursively discovers `pyproject.toml` and `setup.py` beneath the scanner base directory. `setup.py` is parsed statically; it is never imported or executed. If neither build file exists, module naming uses legacy upward `__init__.py` package detection. If build files exist, roots extracted from all usable files are combined and deduplicated. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PackageRootResolver.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/SetupPySourceRoots.java`.

Recognized `pyproject.toml` root conventions are setuptools package discovery `where`; Poetry package `from` or a Poetry-backend `src` convention; Hatch wheel `sources`, then the directory component of wheel `packages`; uv build `module-root`, otherwise `src`; PDM `package-dir`; and Flit module configuration using `src`. A declared `uv_build` backend also selects `src`. Invalid or unreadable TOML and unrecognized layouts yield no root from that file. Each relative root is resolved relative to its build file, not blindly relative to project base. A root containing `__init__.py` is moved upward until the first non-package parent, without traversing above project base. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PyProjectTomlSourceRoots.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/ConfigSourceRoots.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PackageRootResolver.java`.

When build files exist but none supplies a root, precedence is existing project-base `src` and `lib` directories, then configured `sonar.sources`, then project base. Relative paths are normalized; Windows drive-absolute paths remain absolute even on a non-Windows scanner. Package names feed import resolution, project symbols, dependency impact, and selected framework-specific checks. Evidence: same `PackageRootResolver.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PackageResolutionResult.java`.

Indexing first collects package names, builds a project tree, and computes global symbols. A file that fails during this prepass is logged at debug level and omitted from the global table; its ordinary rule pass still attempts parsing and reports the parser failure. Namespace-package analysis is telemetry-only and cannot fail the analysis. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/PythonIndexer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/SonarQubePythonIndexer.java`.

### Incremental analysis

`sonar.python.skipUnchanged` can force optimized analysis when the platform cache is available; platform `canSkipUnchangedFiles` may also enable it. Optimization additionally requires a matching analyzer cache version and unchanged test-source configuration. Cache version includes plugin implementation version and the ordered configured Python-version values. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/SonarQubePythonIndexer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/caching/Caching.java`.

An apparently unchanged file is accepted only when platform status is `SAME` and a constant-time comparison confirms the stored and current content hashes. Cached descriptors and import maps rebuild the project model. Changed, deleted, or cache-incomplete modules are impactful; the dependency graph marks their import dependents. Unchanged unimpacted files may skip parsing entirely. Unchanged impacted files may skip ordinary cached-capable checks but remain available for consumers that need regeneration. A cache miss, failed deserialization, missing effective test type, unsupported check, or missing CPD tokens falls back to parsing rather than guessing. Project descriptors, import maps, file lists, content hashes, effective test types, CPD tokens, and used Typeshed module names are analyzer cache payloads. Evidence: same `SonarQubePythonIndexer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/cpd/PythonCpdAnalyzer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/caching/Caching.java`.

## 8. Processing lifecycle, concurrency, and partial results

Python processing follows these observable stages: collect language-tagged inputs; interpret version and test settings; resolve package roots; build or restore the project symbol and type tables; parse each file; construct syntax, binding, type, and control-flow models; run applicable native and custom checks; run configured cross-file callbacks; collect suppression metadata; emit issues; emit metrics and presentation data; collect telemetry; finalize index/cache and end-of-analysis checks. Notebook server processing first extracts code and mapping, then uses the notebook parser and a cache-disabled index. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/Scanner.java`.

`sonar.python.analysis.parallel` defaults to `true`. When enabled without an explicit thread count, the worker count is rounded from 90% of available processors, bounded to 1 through 6. `sonar.python.analysis.threads` below 1 becomes 1; an explicit positive value is not capped by the analyzer. Disabling parallel analysis forces one worker. Work uses a work-stealing pool. Scanner result writers share a lock, and custom/non-native repositories and end-of-analysis callbacks are serialized per repository. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/Scanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/MeasuresRepository.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IssuesRepository.java`.

Cancellation stops files as workers observe the cancelled context and marks progress cancelled. An ordinary per-file exception is logged, that file contributes no later stage results, and other files continue. With `sonar.internal.analysis.failFast=true`, non-test exceptions abort analysis; a syntax recognition error in a platform test file still remains nonfatal. A syntax recognition error otherwise creates a parser-rule issue and a platform analysis error on the affected file, increments recognition-error telemetry, and permits independent files to complete when fail-fast is off. Evidence: same `Scanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`; `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/ParsingErrorCheck.java`.

Notebook JSON extraction catches each notebook independently. With fail-fast off, malformed or structurally unexpected JSON drops that notebook and increments notebook-exception telemetry. With fail-fast on, a non-test notebook extraction failure aborts; test-notebook extraction remains nonfatal. Unsupported-language notebooks are skipped normally and do not count as exceptions. Notebook syntax recognition failures follow ordinary per-file parser behavior after extraction. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbSensor.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IpynbNotebookParser.java`.

No analyzer-owned durable database state exists. Scanner report data and analyzer cache writes use platform lifecycles. A failed quick-fix conversion does not suppress the issue. Missing cross-file secondary-location files are omitted with debug logging; remaining issue data is still saved. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IssuesRepository.java`; `manifest/dependency-map.md`.


## 9. Issues, suppression, profiles, and quick fixes

Native repository key `python` applies to language `py`; repository key `ipython` applies to language `ipynb`. Both are named `SonarAnalyzer` and define the same 434 Community checks. Rule scope `Main`, `Tests`, or `All` uses effective rule type from Section 3.3. Rule activation and parameter override are platform quality-profile contracts. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonRuleRepository.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbRuleRepository.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/AbstractPythonRuleRepository.java`; `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/OpenSourceCheckList.java`.

`Sonar way` contains 389 native keys for Python. `Sonar agentic AI` contains 306. The notebook variants remove `S905`, `S2201`, `S5754`, and `S1481`, leaving 385 and 302 respectively; those rules remain definable and can be manually activated for notebooks. Evidence: `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/Sonar_way_profile.json`; `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/Sonar_agentic_AI_profile.json`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbRuleRepository.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbProfile.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IPynbAIQualityProfile.java`.

The exact catalog totals are 248 code smells, 124 bugs, and 62 vulnerabilities; scopes are 145 Main, 30 Tests, and 259 All. Metadata status is 422 ready, 8 deprecated, and 4 beta. Quick-fix metadata is 29 covered, 27 targeted, 6 partial, 26 infeasible, and 346 unspecified. Actual text edits are attached only in SonarLint. Failure to create a fix leaves the issue intact. Evidence: exact metadata JSON files under `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IssuesRepository.java`.

The bundled metadata directory contains 437 rule JSON objects, but repository construction is driven by annotated classes, not directory enumeration. `NoSonar` is the registered public key whose internal key is `S1291`; its metadata object carries `sqKey: S1291`. Metadata-only `S1523`, `S4721`, and `S7932` have no class in the exact Community registration list and must not be exposed as shipped Community rules. Thus the 434-entry catalog below is complete for this artifact, not merely a count of resource files. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/AbstractPythonRuleRepository.java`; `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/OpenSourceCheckList.java`; exact metadata directory above.

Bare valid `NOSONAR` or `noqa` comments suppress all suppressible rules on their line. Qualified forms suppress only listed alphanumeric rule IDs. Bare `nosec` suppresses security rules only; valid Bandit-style rule IDs can target specific security rules. Multiple inline suppression fragments are merged, except any bare all-rule suppression wins. Native `S1309` and `NoSonar` are never suppressed by this filter, so use of suppression remains reportable. Rule `S7632` reports malformed suppression syntax. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/api/nosonar/NoSonarInfoParser.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/nosonar/NoSonarIssueFilter.java`; `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/NoSonarCommentFormatCheck.java`.

### 9.1 Complete native rule catalog

Catalog field meanings: scope is the effective file-type gate; status and quick-fix classification come from exact shipped metadata; profile membership is exact release membership; “none” under parameters means no `@RuleProperty`; exclusions describe rule-specific guards in addition to activation and scope. Each source path names the exact immutable source artifact member used to specify detection and issue location.

Location: For DMS without publicly_accessible, the resource call is primary; explicit public-access settings are located on their argument flow, RDS public subnet selection is located on the subnet argument flow, EC2 Instance public subnet selection is located on the containing vpc_subnets argument or dictionary pair, and EC2 CfnInstance findings are located on the associate_public_ip_address argument or dictionary pair. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/cdk/PublicNetworkAccessToCloudResourcesCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S6329.json`.

Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/UselessPytestMarksCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9074.json`.
- **`S9075`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata unspecified; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Reports a pytest warns assertion with no expected-warning argument or with Warning as a type or object anywhere in an expected-warning list or tuple when no effective message match is supplied. Exclusions: Only calls recognized as pytest warns are applicable; a match argument whose expression is not falsy excludes the call, and supplied warning arguments containing no Warning type or object are excluded. Location: The pytest warns call expression when the expected-warning argument is absent; otherwise, the matched broad Warning expression, including the first such element found recursively in a list or tuple. Parameters: none. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/SpecificWarningAssertionCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9075.json`.
- **`S9076`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata unspecified; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Detects a decorator, whether used directly or called, whose relevant expression resolves to pytest.yield_fixture. Exclusions: Decorators are excluded when the decorator expression itself, or the callee when the expression is a call, does not resolve to pytest.yield_fixture. Location: The primary issue is located on the entire decorator expression. Parameters: none. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/DeprecatedYieldFixtureCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9076.json`.
- **`S9077`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata unspecified; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Reports a literal false-or-zero assert when the file imports pytest, and reports a pytest.fail call whose reason, first positional message, or msg argument is absent or is an empty or whitespace-only string literal. Exclusions: Assert checks are excluded unless the file imports pytest; parenthesized assert conditions qualify only when they reduce to the name False or a numeric literal whose integer value is zero; pytest.fail calls are excluded when a selected message argument is present and is not an empty or whitespace-only string literal. Location: The entire assert statement for a literal false-or-zero assertion, or the entire pytest.fail call for a missing message. Parameters: none. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/IntentionalTestFailureMessageCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9077.json`.
- **`S9078`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata unspecified; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Detects each parameter case in a recognized parametrize decorator that is structurally equivalent to an earlier case after removing parentheses. Exclusions: Decorators are excluded when the delegated parametrize argument lookup finds no values expression or when that expression is neither a list nor a tuple. Location: The primary issue is located on the duplicate test-case expression, with the first equivalent case marked as secondary. Parameters: none. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/DuplicateParametrizeCasesCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9078.json`.
- **`S9083`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata targeted; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Flags an argument-free pytest fixture or mark decorator when its use of empty parentheses differs from the configured requireParentheses style. Exclusions: Only decorators resolved as pytest.fixture, a known pytest mark, or a qualified member of pytest.mark are checked, and decorator calls containing arguments are excluded from the empty-parentheses check. Location: When parentheses are forbidden, the primary issue spans the empty parentheses; when parentheses are required, it is located on the decorator expression. Parameters: `requireParentheses` default `false`. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/PytestDecoratorParenthesesCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9083.json`.
- **`S9084`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata unspecified; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Flags an absolute from-import whose module begins with pytest and a direct import of such a module when it is aliased to any name other than pytest. Exclusions: Relative from-imports, direct imports without aliases, and direct imports aliased exactly as pytest are excluded. Location: For a from-import, the primary issue is located on the entire import statement; for an aliased direct import, it is located on the aliased module entry. Parameters: none. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/PytestModuleImportCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9084.json`.
- **`S9116`** — CODE_SMELL; scope Tests; status ready; quick-fix metadata unspecified; built-in profiles Sonar way. Detection: Flags the first positional regular argument or single-star unpacking argument passed to a called pytest.fixture decorator. Exclusions: Decorators that are not calls resolved as pytest.fixture, keyword arguments, and double-star unpacking arguments are excluded. Location: The primary issue is located on the first offending decorator argument. Parameters: none. Evidence: `python-checks-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/checks/tests/PytestFixturePositionalArgsCheck.java` and `sonar-python-plugin-5.27.0.35548.jar!/org/sonar/l10n/py/rules/python/S9116.json`.

## 10. Metrics, highlighting, symbols, and duplication

For parsed platform-main Python or notebook files outside SonarLint, the analyzer emits `ncloc`, `statements`, `functions`, `classes`, `complexity`, `cognitive_complexity`, and `comment_lines`. Platform-test files receive file-level `ncloc` only. Docstring lines are removed from code and executable-line sets and counted as comment lines. A nonblank lexical comment counts once per line unless it contains `NOSONAR`; punctuation-only comment text is blank. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/MeasuresRepository.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/FileMetrics.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/FileLinesVisitor.java`.

Statement count covers executable syntax categories registered by `FileLinesVisitor`; function count includes nested definitions; class count counts class definitions. Cyclomatic complexity increments for function definitions, loops, non-`elif` conditionals, conditional expressions, Boolean `and`/`or`, and comprehension conditions. Per-function complexity excludes nested function contributions. Cognitive complexity follows the analyzer’s nesting-aware visitor and is emitted as the core cognitive-complexity measure. Evidence: `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/ComplexityVisitor.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/CognitiveComplexityVisitor.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/FileMetrics.java`.

For ordinary Python, the analyzer emits per-line `ncloc_data` and `executable_lines_data`. Notebook file-level counts are computed from generated code and projected tokens, but notebook `ncloc_data` is omitted and executable lines are empty. The coverage importer, not executable-line generation, owns observed hits and branch conditions. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/MeasuresRepository.java`; `python-frontend-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/python/metrics/FileLinesVisitor.java`.

Highlighting classifies numbers as constants, language keywords as keywords, strings as strings, module/function/class docstrings as structured comments, and lexical comments as comments. `async`, `await`, `match`, and `case` are highlighted as keywords even where lexer compatibility treats them as identifiers. Highlighting is emitted for parsed files outside SonarLint and projected to notebook JSON coordinates through the notebook tree. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonHighlighter.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`.

Symbol output includes global variables, class and instance fields, function and lambda locals, and comprehension locals. A symbol without usages is omitted. The earliest source-ordered usage is the declaration anchor and later usages are references. Symbol-table saving is serialized. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/NewSymbolsCollector.java`.

CPD is emitted only for ordinary Python platform-main files outside SonarLint. Newline, indent, dedent, and end-of-file tokens are normally omitted; a newline immediately before a dedent is retained so indentation changes remain distinguishable. Token values and precise ranges are submitted to the platform. Serialized CPD tokens can be restored from incremental cache; corrupt or incomplete cached data triggers a full parse. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/cpd/PythonCpdAnalyzer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonScanner.java`.

## 11. Coverage and test-execution reports

### 11.1 Cobertura coverage

`sonar.python.coverage.reportPaths` is a project multi-value setting accepting relative Ant-style patterns or absolute paths. When absent, default `coverage-reports/*coverage-*.xml` is searched. Explicit entries are each resolved; duplicate absolute report paths are read once. Removed key `sonar.python.coverage.reportPath` produces a warning and does not replace the plural-key/default selection. The sensor applies only to language `py`, not `ipynb`. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/coverage/PythonCoverageSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonReportSensor.java`.

A Cobertura report supplies optional `sources`, `packages`, `class filename`, and line records. Existing absolute source directories are accepted; relative sources resolve under scanner base. If no valid source remains, scanner base is used. An absolute filename must exist. A relative filename must resolve under exactly one source directory; no match or multiple matches drops that file’s coverage and records an import warning. Each line emits hits. A branch line with nonblank condition coverage emits covered and total conditions parsed from the parenthesized ratio. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/coverage/CoberturaParser.java`.

Empty reports are ignored with a warning. XML, numeric, path, or other failures are isolated per report; already imported reports remain, later reports continue, and an analysis warning identifies the failed report. Parser-level nonfatal path errors retain resolvable files. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/coverage/PythonCoverageSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/coverage/CoberturaParser.java`.

### 11.2 xUnit/JUnit-report test execution

`sonar.python.xunit.reportPath` accepts a path or Ant-style pattern relative to project base; default is `xunit-reports/xunit-result-*.xml`. `sonar.python.xunit.skipDetails` defaults to `false`. The sensor applies to Python main analysis and parses descendant `testsuite` elements and direct `testcase` children. A test case requires `name` and `time`; optional `file` and `classname` support source association. Its first child determines skipped, failure, or error state. Time is converted from seconds to milliseconds. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/xunit/PythonXUnitSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/xunit/TestSuiteParser.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`.

Detailed mode first resolves the testcase `file`; otherwise it converts suite or classname dotted names to Python test paths, with a second attempt that removes a final class segment. Only indexed test files match. It emits `tests`, `skipped_tests`, `test_errors`, `test_failures`, and `test_execution_time` on each resolved file. Unresolved tests are warned and omitted. Simple mode aggregates the same metrics at module level and is intended for reports whose paths cannot be resolved. If no tests exist, it emits nothing. Malformed required attributes, time values, or XML abort that report-processing invocation and produce one report warning through the generic report sensor; measures saved before failure are not rolled back by this component. Evidence: same xUnit sources; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonReportSensor.java`.

## 12. External issue report import

External import sensors are absent in SonarLint and run only when their configuration key is present, except deprecated Pylint singular-key compatibility described below. They read reports; they do not run the external tool. Each report is isolated on read, parse, or runtime failure. Unresolved input paths drop those issues; the final warning lists at most 20 sorted paths and an ellipsis when more exist. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/ExternalIssuesSensor.java`.

Pylint uses `sonar.python.pylint.reportPaths`; deprecated `sonar.python.pylint.reportPath` is used only when plural-key report resolution returns no files, with a deprecation warning. Flake8 uses `sonar.python.flake8.reportPaths`. Their normal text format carries file, line, column, rule, and message; the supported legacy format omits columns and permits a parenthesized symbolic suffix after the rule ID. Pylint columns are zero-based; Flake8 columns are converted from one-based. Invalid lines are ignored. Imported issues are code smells with Major severity and five minutes remediation; an out-of-bounds or missing column becomes a whole-line location. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/pylint/PylintSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/flake8/Flake8Sensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/TextReportReader.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/ExternalIssuesSensor.java`.

Mypy uses `sonar.python.mypy.reportPaths`. Only `error` records are imported. It accepts start line, optional start column, and optional end line/column; columns are converted from one-based. Missing error code becomes `unknown_mypy_rule`. Rule IDs longer than 200 characters are dropped. Messages longer than 500 characters are truncated to 500 characters plus an ellipsis. A missing or invalid end range falls back through generic single-column or whole-line behavior. Imported issues are Major code smells with five minutes remediation. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/mypy/MypySensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/ExternalIssuesSensor.java`.

Bandit uses `sonar.python.bandit.reportPaths` and reads the JSON root `results` array. Each result needs nonempty `test_id`, `filename`, and `issue_text`; optional `line_number`, `issue_severity`, and `issue_confidence` determine location and severity. Imported issues are vulnerabilities with five minutes remediation. High severity plus high confidence is Blocker; other high severity is Critical; medium is Major; all other values are Minor. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/bandit/BanditJsonReportReader.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/bandit/BanditSensor.java`.

Ruff uses `sonar.python.ruff.reportPaths` and reads a JSON array whose entries carry `code`, `filename`, `message`, `location`, and `end_location`. Imported issues are Major code smells with five minutes remediation. Direct indexed-path lookup has precedence. If it fails, normalized report path suffixes are matched against indexed relative paths sharing the filename; longest match wins and equal-length ambiguity drops the issue. Valid ranges use Ruff rows and columns with end-column adjustment; invalid or incomplete ranges use the start line. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/ruff/RuffJsonReportReader.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/ruff/RuffSensor.java`.

## 13. Configuration catalog and precedence

| Key | Default | Scope and behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `sonar.python.file.suffixes` | `py` | Project multi-value Python suffix list; trim blanks; all-blank restores default. |
| `sonar.ipynb.file.suffixes` | `ipynb` | Project multi-value notebook suffix list; trim blanks; all-blank restores default. |
| `sonar.python.version` | all models `3.8`–`3.14` | Project multi-value semantic/version compatibility set; Section 4 defines fallback and clamping. |
| `sonar.python.analysis.parallel` | `true` | Enables analyzer parallel file processing. |
| `sonar.python.analysis.threads` | derived | Positive explicit worker count; values below 1 become 1; ignored when parallel is false. |
| `sonar.python.skipUnchanged` | platform decision | `true` can force incremental unchanged-file optimization when cache validity gates pass; `false` does not override a platform `canSkipUnchangedFiles` decision. |
| `sonar.python.testFileHeuristic.disabled` | `false` | Disables analyzer fallback test classification. Nonblank `sonar.tests` also has precedence over the heuristic. |
| `sonar.python.coverage.reportPaths` | `coverage-reports/*coverage-*.xml` | Project multi-value Cobertura report patterns/paths. Explicit key replaces default search. |
| `sonar.python.coverage.reportPath` | removed | Emits migration warning; use plural key. |
| `sonar.python.xunit.reportPath` | `xunit-reports/xunit-result-*.xml` | xUnit/JUnit-report path or pattern. |
| `sonar.python.xunit.skipDetails` | `false` | `true` emits module aggregates instead of file detail. |
| `sonar.python.pylint.reportPaths` | absent | Enables Pylint import. |
| `sonar.python.pylint.reportPath` | absent, deprecated | Used only as Pylint fallback when plural-key report resolution is empty. |
| `sonar.python.flake8.reportPaths` | absent | Enables Flake8 import. |
| `sonar.python.mypy.reportPaths` | absent | Enables Mypy import. |
| `sonar.python.bandit.reportPaths` | absent | Enables Bandit import. |
| `sonar.python.ruff.reportPaths` | absent | Enables Ruff import. |
| `sonar.python.performance.measure` | `false` | Internal performance report activation. |
| `sonar.python.performance.measure.path` | work-directory `sonar-python-performance-measure.json` when available | Internal performance report destination. |
| `sonar.python.sonarlint.indexing.maxlines` | `300000` | SonarLint-only maximum total project lines before project-level symbol indexing is disabled. |
| `sonar.internal.analysis.failFast` | `false` | Internal failure policy described in Section 8. |

Rule parameters and defaults appear on every parameterized rule in Section 9.1. Quality-profile activation and project/branch setting precedence are platform contracts; this analyzer reads the effective scanner configuration. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/Scanner.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonSensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/TestFileClassifier.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/SonarQubePythonIndexer.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/SonarLintPythonIndexer.java`; report sensors cited above.

Dependency telemetry reads indexed files named exactly `requirements.txt` and `pyproject.toml`, merges and post-processes parsed dependency names, joins names as format version `1`, and emits no analysis issue or dependency graph result. Import telemetry records sorted imported module names as format version `1`. Parse gaps affect telemetry only. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/DependencyTelemetrySensor.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/dependency/DependencyTelemetry.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/dependency/RequirementsTxtParser.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/dependency/PyProjectTomlParser.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/telemetry/collectors/ImportsTelemetryCollector.java`.

## 15. Security and trust boundaries

Analyzed Python, notebook code, `setup.py`, and report content are untrusted data. The analyzer lexes and parses them; it does not import modules, start Python, execute notebook magics, evaluate `setup.py`, invoke Pylint/Flake8/Mypy/Bandit/Ruff, or follow runtime application behavior. Static source-root extraction accepts only recognized literal structure. Evidence: parser and indexer sources cited in Sections 4 and 7; external sensor sources cited in Section 12.

Notebook JSON is streamed through a JSON parser and only selected top-level metadata/cell fields are interpreted. XML reports use analyzer-commons `SafeStaxParserFactory`, low-memory StAX configuration, and a local undeclared-entity resolver; malformed XML is handled as a report failure rather than project code. JSON external reports accept only expected scalar fields and never execute contents. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/parser/StaxParser.java`; notebook and external JSON readers cited above.

Report file paths and source roots cross filesystem trust boundaries. Coverage accepts only existing uniquely resolved files. Generic external import requires an indexed path. Ruff’s suffix fallback rejects equal-best ambiguity but remains best-effort when unrelated files share a suffix; consumers must generate reports against the same source tree when exact attribution matters. External issue messages are treated as report text; Mypy alone imposes the analyzer-owned 500-character truncation described above. Evidence: coverage and external sensors cited above.

Suppression comments are a deliberate trust boundary: bare all-rule suppression does not disable `S1309` or `NoSonar`, and bare `nosec` is restricted to security-classified rule keys. Security classification is derived from native rule metadata. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/nosonar/NoSonarIssueFilter.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/nosonar/SecurityRuleKeyProvider.java`.

## 16. Compatibility and limits

The plugin requires platform language keys `py` and `ipynb`, Sonar plugin API compatibility at least `9.9`, and JRE at least `11`; this release is consumed by Community Build `26.8.0.126808`. SonarLint support is declared, but server-only coverage, xUnit, external report, and dependency telemetry extensions are not installed in SonarLint. Quick fixes are emitted only there. Evidence: runtime manifest; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/PythonExtensions.java`; `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/IssuesRepository.java`.

Server analysis has no analyzer-owned source-file size or line-count ceiling in the inspected exact sources. [INFERENCE] Scanner/platform memory, report, and file limits still bound operation. SonarLint project-level indexing alone has the explicit default 300,000-total-line ceiling; exceeding it disables cross-file indexing but not per-file analysis. Evidence: `python-commons-5.27.0.35548-sources.jar!/org/sonar/plugins/python/indexer/SonarLintPythonIndexer.java`.

Static semantics are intentionally partial. Dynamic imports, monkey patching, metaprogramming, conditional runtime definitions, unavailable third-party stubs, and unresolved inheritance can produce unknown or ambiguous symbols. Cataloged rules generally skip findings when their required semantic proof is unavailable. A successful parse is not a claim that code runs on every configured Python version. Evidence: semantic and type-model sources cited in Section 6; per-rule exclusions in Section 9.1.

Notebook-specific limits are intentional: no server incremental cache, project-level cross-file symbols, CPD, or executable-line data; only code cells; exact lowercase language metadata recognition; and physical JSON-coordinate results. Coverage, xUnit, and external-linter sensors are language-`py` integrations and do not remap ordinary report coordinates into notebook JSON. Evidence: Sections 3, 5, 10, 11, and 12.

## 17. Acceptance scenarios

1. **Default Python recognition.** Given an indexed source file ending in `.py` and no suffix override, when analysis runs, expected outcome is language `py`, parsing, applicable native rules, Python measures, highlighting, symbols, and main-file CPD. A file with another suffix is not claimed by this analyzer.
2. **Blank suffix override.** Given `sonar.python.file.suffixes` containing only blank values, when language registration occurs, expected outcome is restoration of suffix `py`, not an empty language definition.
3. **Version omitted.** Given no `sonar.python.version` in server analysis, when Python files are analyzed, expected outcome is all models `3.8` through `3.14`, one configuration warning, and version-filtered Typeshed symbols from their union.
4. **Version fallback and suffix handling.** Given a version value with valid major and minor plus any trailing component, when the setting is parsed, expected outcome is the mapped major/minor model and an unsupported-version warning; the trailing component is ignored even when malformed. Given a multi-value setting with a malformed major or minor member, expected outcome is all supported models rather than a partially accumulated subset. Given a numeric version above `3.14`, expected outcome is model `3.14` plus a warning.
5. **Combined grammar.** Given syntax represented in the exact legacy/current grammar but incompatible with a selected runtime version, when parsing runs, expected outcome is a syntax tree; configured-version rules may report incompatibility. Parser acceptance alone must not reject it solely because of the setting.
6. **Fallback test detection.** Given no `sonar.tests`, a platform-main file under a `tests` directory, and both Main- and Tests-scoped rules active, when rules run, expected outcome is Tests-scope execution, no Main-scope execution, one project warning at most, and metric treatment still based on platform-main type.
7. **Ordinary parse failure.** Given one malformed main Python file, another valid file, and fail-fast disabled, when analysis runs, expected outcome is a `ParsingError` issue and platform analysis error for the malformed file while the valid file completes. With fail-fast enabled, expected outcome is analysis abort after that non-test failure.
8. **Python notebook mapping.** Given a valid `.ipynb` with Python metadata, code and markdown cells, escaped strings, and compressed JSON, when analysis runs, expected outcome is analysis of code cells only and issue/highlight/symbol ranges mapped to physical notebook JSON coordinates.
9. **Non-Python notebook.** Given notebook metadata naming only an unsupported language, when notebook extraction runs, expected outcome is a normal skip with no Python issue and no notebook-exception count.
10. **Malformed notebook.** Given invalid notebook JSON and fail-fast disabled, when extraction runs, expected outcome is omission of that notebook, incremented notebook-exception telemetry, and continued processing of independent notebooks. Fail-fast aborts only when the notebook is not platform-test.
11. **Notebook profile.** Given default `Sonar way` for `ipynb`, when profile creation runs, expected outcome is 385 active keys and omission of `S905`, `S2201`, `S5754`, and `S1481`; each remains available for explicit activation.
12. **Parameterized native rule.** Given active `S3776` with no override, when a function’s cognitive complexity exceeds 15, expected outcome is an issue at the cataloged location. Given a profile override, expected outcome uses that threshold under platform setting precedence.
13. **Targeted suppression.** Given a valid suppression comment listing one rule key on an issue line, when native issues are filtered, expected outcome is suppression of that key only. Bare `nosec` suppresses security rules only; `S1309`, `NoSonar`, and malformed-suppression rule `S7632` remain observable according to their definitions.
14. **Incremental unchanged file.** Given valid matching cache metadata, unchanged test settings, platform status `SAME`, a matching content hash, and no dependency impact, when analysis runs, expected outcome is restored symbols and CPD with no parse. Missing CPD or effective-type cache data causes a full parse.
15. **Coverage resolution.** Given a Cobertura class filename resolving under exactly one valid source directory, when imported, expected outcome is line hits and any parsed branch conditions on that indexed file. Zero or multiple resolutions drop only that file’s coverage and emit a warning.
16. **Empty or malformed coverage.** Given one valid and one malformed coverage report with fail-fast unrelated to report import, when coverage runs, expected outcome is retention of valid report measures and a warning for the malformed report.
17. **xUnit detail modes.** Given resolvable testcase paths and default settings, when xUnit import runs, expected outcome is test counts, failures, errors, skips, and duration on test files. With `sonar.python.xunit.skipDetails=true`, expected outcome is module-level aggregate metrics instead.
18. **Mypy boundaries.** Given an `error` record without a code, when imported, expected outcome is rule ID `unknown_mypy_rule`. A non-error record or a rule ID longer than 200 characters produces no issue; an overlong message is truncated as specified.
19. **Bandit severity.** Given a complete Bandit result with high severity and high confidence, when imported, expected outcome is a Blocker vulnerability with five minutes remediation at its reported line. Missing rule, path, or message drops the record.
20. **Ruff path fallback.** Given an absolute report path from another base directory whose longest indexed relative suffix is unique, when direct lookup fails, expected outcome is attachment to that indexed file. Equal-best suffix candidates cause omission as ambiguous.
21. **Parallel execution.** Given parallel enabled and no explicit thread setting on an eight-processor runtime, when worker count is derived, expected outcome is no more than six workers. Given explicit `12`, expected outcome is 12 analyzer workers; disabling parallel forces one.
22. **Cancellation.** Given scanner cancellation during a file set, when workers observe cancellation, expected outcome is cancelled progress and no new work for those files; already saved independent results are not rolled back by this analyzer.
23. **Rule-catalog completeness.** Given exact Community artifacts, when repository construction is audited, expected outcome is exactly 434 registered keys in both native repositories, with `NoSonar` internally mapped to `S1291`; metadata-only `S1523`, `S4721`, and `S7932` are not registered.

Evidence: acceptance outcomes trace to the exact sections and source paths cited above.
